Replace any old artifacts/packages that reference `javax` with those that reference `jakarta`.  \\nExample:\\n```xml\\n<dependency>\\n  <groupId>org.geotools</groupId>\\n  <artifactId>gt-main</artifactId>\\n  <version>30.0</version>\\n</dependency>\\n```\\n\\n### b. **Update Imports in Your Code**\\nSearch and replace all imports:\\n- `javax.xml.bind.*` → `jakarta.xml.bind.*`\\n- `javax.servlet.*` → `jakarta.servlet.*`\\n- `javax.activation.*` → `jakarta.activation.*`\\n- ...and any other Java EE imports as needed\\n\\nYour IDE can typically help automate this refactor.\\n\\n### c. **Check Third-party Libraries**\\nEnsure all libraries you depend on have Jakarta-compatible versions. If not, seek updates or Jakarta-forked versions.\\n\\n### d. **Review Your Build/Runtime Environment**\\nMake sure your application server (if any) supports the Jakarta EE 9+ namespace, e.g., Tomcat 10+. If you use a container that supports only `javax.*`, GeoTools 30 will not work.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 4. **Common Problems and Fixes**\\n- **ClassNotFoundException for old javax classes:** Update all code and libraries for Jakarta imports.\\n- **XML, JAXB, or activation errors:** Use Jakarta versions.\\n- **GeoServer plugin/extension issues:** Rebuild plugins/extensions against the GeoTools 30 (Jakarta) APIs.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 5. **References & Guides**\\n\\n- [GeoTools 30 Release Notes](https://geotoolsnews.blogspot.com/2023/10/geotools-30.html)\\n- [Official GeoTools Migration Guide](https://docs.geotools.org/latest/userguide/about/migration.html)\\n- [Jakarta EE Migration Tool](https://github.com/eclipse/transformer) – automates package refactoring from `javax` to `jakarta`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 6. **Example: Code Migration**\\n\\n**Before (GeoTools <30):**\\n```java\\nimport javax.xml.bind.JAXBContext;\\nimport javax.servlet.ServletContext;\\n```\\n\\n**After your code, configuration, and third-party dependencies from `javax.*` to `jakarta.*` namespaces to align with Jakarta EE 9+ changes  .\\n\\nIf you run into specific migration errors, please share the The Java EE APIs moved from `javax.*` to `jakarta.*` starting with Jakarta EE 9. This has an impact on projects dependent on these APIs, including GeoTools modules that use things like JAXB, Servlet, or JAX-RS.\\n\\nHere's a **breakdown with example guidance** for updating your Ant build script and code:\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 1. **Update Dependencies**\\n\\nIf you use Ant's `<dependency>` tasks or a system that fetches jars (like Ivy or Ant + Maven), you need to replace old Java EE (javax) coordinates with the new Jakarta ones.\\n\\n**Example: JAXB (XML Binding)**\\n- **Old Maven/Gradle coords:**  \\n  `javax.xml.bind:jaxb-api`\\n- **New Maven/Gradle coords (Jakarta):**  \\n  `jakarta.xml.bind:jakarta.xml.bind-api`\\n\\n**For Ant (Ivy-style example):**\\n```xml\\n<dependency org=\\\"jakarta.xml.bind\\\" name=\\\"jakarta.xml.bind-api\\\" rev=\\\"3.0.1\\\" />\\n```\\n\\n**Manual JAR handling:**  \\nDownload and place the appropriate Jakarta JARs in your `lib` and update your classpath in `build.xml`.  \\n**Example:**\\n```xml\\n<path id=\\\"project.classpath\\\">\\n    <pathelement location=\\\"lib/jakarta.xml.bind-api-3.0.1.jar\\\"/>\\n    <pathelement location=\\\"lib/other-dependencies.jar\\\"/>\\n</path>\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. **Update Your Java Source Code**\\n\\nChange imports:\\n- `javax.xml.bind.*` → `jakarta.xml.bind.*`\\n- `javax.servlet.*` → `jakarta.servlet.*`\\n- etc.\\n\\n**You can do this using:**\\n- IDE refactoring tools (Recommended)\\n- Batch search/replace\\n- Tools like [OpenRewrite](https://github.com/openrewrite/rewrite)-based recipes or [Eclipse Transformer](https://projects.eclipse.org/projects/technology.transformer)\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 3. **GeoTools-specific Concerns**\\n\\n- GeoTools core is not directly dependent on Jakarta EE, but extensions for WMS, WFS, or XML binding might be.\\n- Check which modules are affected (typically, XML-related or web-related).\\n- If you're using prebuilt GeoTools JARs, ensure you use a Jakarta-enabled distribution (GeoTools 28.x+).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 4. **Ant Tasks Example: Compilation**\\n\\nMake sure to set the `source` and `target` properties to match your JDK version (Java 11+ recommended for Jakarta).\\n\\n```xml\\n<javac srcdir=\\\"src\\\" destdir=\\\"build/classes\\\" classpathref=\\\"project.classpath\\\"\\n       source=\\\"11\\\" target=\\\"11\\\"/>\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 5. **Summary Table**\\n\\n| Old `javax` dependency (example)    | New `jakarta` dependency (example)            |\\n|-------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------|\\n| javax.xml.bind:jaxb-api             | jakarta.xml.bind:jakarta.xml.bind-api         |\\n| javax.servlet:javax.servlet-api     | jakarta.servlet:jakarta.servlet-api           |\\n| javax.activation:javax.activation-api| jakarta.activation:jakarta.activation-api     |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 6. **References & Tools**\\n\\n- [Eclipse Transformer (namespace rewrite)](https://github.com/eclipse/transformer)\\n- [GeoTools Jakarta Migration Issue This page collects the upgrade notes for each release change; highlighting any fundamental changes with code examples showing how to upgrade your code.\n\nThe first step to upgrade: change the `geotools.version` of your dependencies in your `pom.xml` to 34.0:\n\n<properties>\n    <project.build.sourceEncoding>UTF-8</project.build.sourceEncoding>\n    <geotools.version>34.0</geotools.version>\n</properties>\n....\n<dependencies>\n      <dependency>\n        <groupId>org.geotools</groupId>\n        <artifactId>gt-bom</artifactId>\n        <version>${geotools.version}</version>\n        <type>pom</type>\n        <scope>import</scope>\n      </dependency>\n    <dependency>\n        <groupId>org.geotools</groupId>\n        <artifactId>gt-shapefile</artifactId>\n    </dependency>\n    <dependency>\n        <groupId>org.geotools</groupId>\n        <artifactId>gt-swing</artifactId>\n    </dependency>\n    ....\n</dependencies>\n\n## GeoTools 35.x[¶](#geotools-35-x \"Link to this heading\")\n\n### JavaEE to JakartaEE Migration[¶](#javaee-to-jakartaee-migration \"Link to this heading\")\n\nGeoTools 35.x has upgraded from JavaEE to JakartaEE reflecting the transition of this technology from Oracle to the Eclipse Foundation. Jakarta version mangement is provided by platform-dependencies/pom.xml import.\n\nBEFORE:\n\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>javax.xml.bind</groupId>\n  <artifactId>jaxb-api</artifactId>\n</dependency>\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>javax.activation</groupId>\n  <artifactId>javax.activation-api</artifactId>\n</dependency>\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>javax.mail</groupId>\n  <artifactId>mail</artifactId>\n</dependency>\n\nimport javax.xml.bind.annotation.XmlRootElement;\n\nAFTER:\n\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>jakarta.xml.bind</groupId>\n  <artifactId>jakarta.xml.bind-api</artifactId>\n</dependency>\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>jakarta.activation</groupId>\n  <artifactId>jakarta.activation-api</artifactId>\n</dependency>\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>jakarta.mail</groupId>\n  <artifactId>jakarta.mail-api</artifactId>\n</dependency>\n\nimport jakarta.xml.bind.annotation.XmlRootElement;\n\nMigration instructions:\n\n*   [Migrate to Jakarta EE 10](https://docs.openrewrite.org/recipes/java/migrate/jakarta/jakartaee10\\)) (Open Rewrite Script)\n    \n\n### DefaultEntityResolver[¶](#defaultentityresolver \"Link to this heading\")\n\n`GeoTools.getEntityResolver(Hints)` has been updated to return `` DefaultEntityResolver` `` by default. This implementation allows access to commonly used OGC and INSPIRE schema locations.\n\nWe recommend this default, however you may configure GeoTools with your own more permissive EntityResolver:\n\n### NetCDF Index removal[¶](#netcdf-index-removal \"Link to this heading\")\n\nStarting with GeoTools 35.x, NetCDF plugin (and coverage multidim) has been simplified by removing external indexing mechanisms. In particular, it no longer relies on\n\n*   a database (e.g., H2 or PostGIS) to map temporal and elevation domains to image indices,\n    \n*   nor on the auxiliary .idx binary files previously used to link image indices to NetCDF dimension coordinates.\n    \n\nThese relationships are now resolved directly from the NetCDF structure at runtime, reducing configuration overhead, improving portability, and eliminating synchronization issues between datasets and external indexes.\n\nThe NetCDF plugin no longer generates the legacy binary .idx files and the embedded H2 database previously used for indexing temporal and elevation domains. All indexing is now handled in-memory and derived directly from the NetCDF dataset structure at runtime, eliminating the need to manage or clean up external index artifacts.\n\nDue to that, several methods have been removed/updated.\n\n#### CoverageSlicesCatalog[¶](#coverageslicescatalog \"Link to this heading\")\n\nBEFORE:\n\npublic CoverageSlicesCatalog(final String database, final File parentLocation);\npublic CoverageSlicesCatalog(final String database, final File parentLocation, Repository repository);\npublic CoverageSlicesCatalog(DataStoreConfiguration datastoreConfig);\npublic CoverageSlicesCatalog(DataStoreConfiguration datastoreConfig, Repository repository);\npublic void removeGranules(String typeName, Filter filter, Transaction transaction);\npublic void addGranule(final String typeName, final SimpleFeature granule, final Transaction transaction);\npublic void addGranules(final String typeName, final SimpleFeatureCollection granules, final Transaction transaction);\npublic void createType(SimpleFeatureType featureType);\npublic void createType(String identification, String typeSpec);\npublic List<CoverageSlice\\> getGranules(Query q);\npublic void purge(Filter filter);\n\nAFTER:\n\nAll methods supporting a physical datastore and related operations have been removed. Jakarta version mangement is provided by platform-dependencies/pom.xml import.\n\nBEFORE:\n\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>javax.xml.bind</groupId>\n  <artifactId>jaxb-api</artifactId>\n</dependency>\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>javax.activation</groupId>\n  <artifactId>javax.activation-api</artifactId>\n</dependency>\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>javax.mail</groupId>\n  <artifactId>mail</artifactId>\n</dependency>\n\nimport javax.xml.bind.annotation.XmlRootElement;\n\nAFTER:\n\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>jakarta.xml.bind</groupId>\n  <artifactId>jakarta.xml.bind-api</artifactId>\n</dependency>\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>jakarta.activation</groupId>\n  <artifactId>jakarta.activation-api</artifactId>\n</dependency>\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>jakarta.mail</groupId>\n  <artifactId>jakarta.mail-api</artifactId>\n</dependency>\n\nimport jakarta.xml.bind.annotation.XmlRootElement;\n\nMigration instructions:\n\n*   [Migrate to Jakarta EE 10](https://docs.openrewrite.org/recipes/java/migrate/jakarta/jakartaee10\\)) (Open Rewrite Script)\n    \n\n### DefaultEntityResolver[¶](#defaultentityresolver \"Link to this heading\")\n\n`GeoTools.getEntityResolver(Hints)` has been updated to return `` DefaultEntityResolver` `` by default. This implementation allows access to commonly used OGC and INSPIRE schema locations.\n\nWe recommend this default, however you may configure GeoTools with your own more permissive EntityResolver:\n\n### NetCDF Index removal[¶](#netcdf-index-removal \"Link to this heading\")\n\nStarting with GeoTools 35.x, NetCDF plugin (and coverage multidim) has been simplified by removing external indexing mechanisms. In particular, it no longer relies on\n\n*   a database (e.g., H2 or PostGIS) to map temporal and elevation domains to image indices,\n    \n*   nor on the auxiliary .idx binary files previously used to link image indices to NetCDF dimension coordinates.\n    \n\nThese relationships are now resolved directly from the NetCDF structure at runtime, reducing configuration overhead, improving portability, and eliminating synchronization issues between datasets and external indexes.\n\nThe NetCDF plugin no longer generates the legacy binary .idx files and the embedded H2 database previously used for indexing temporal and elevation domains. All indexing is now handled in-memory and derived directly from the NetCDF dataset structure at runtime, eliminating the need to manage or clean up external index artifacts.\n\nDue to that, several methods have been removed/updated.\n\n#### CoverageSlicesCatalog[¶](#coverageslicescatalog \"Link to this heading\")\n\nBEFORE:\n\npublic CoverageSlicesCatalog(final String database, final File parentLocation);\npublic CoverageSlicesCatalog(final String database, final File parentLocation, Repository repository);\npublic CoverageSlicesCatalog(DataStoreConfiguration datastoreConfig);\npublic CoverageSlicesCatalog(DataStoreConfiguration datastoreConfig, Repository repository);\npublic void removeGranules(String typeName, Filter filter, Transaction transaction);\npublic void addGranule(final String typeName, final SimpleFeature granule, final Transaction transaction);\npublic void addGranules(final String typeName, final SimpleFeatureCollection granules, final Transaction transaction);\npublic void createType(SimpleFeatureType featureType);\npublic void createType(String identification, String typeSpec);\npublic List<CoverageSlice\\> getGranules(Query q);\npublic void purge(Filter filter);\n\nAFTER:\n\nAll methods supporting a physical datastore and related operations have been removed. This was done following a request from the OGC members who manage the GeoAPI project, which uses the \\\"org.opengis\\\" package space. Any code using GeoTools will need to refactor imports and references accordingly.\\n\\nThe key steps and impacts are:\\n\\n**1. Package Refactoring:**\\n- All `org.opengis` imports and usage must be changed to `org.geotools.api` (e.g., `org.opengis.geometry.Envelope` is now `org.geotools.api.geometry.Bounds`).\\n- Other changes include moving the core data API from `org.geotools.data` to `org.geotools.api.data`.\\n\\n**2. Migration Support:**\\n- The GeoTools team has provided migration scripts to help automate these changes, which have been successfully used for major projects like GeoServer and GeoWebCache. Only minor manual edits were required for some large projects.\\n- Full upgrade instructions, including the script, are available in the [GeoTools upgrade guide](https://docs.geotools.org/latest/userguide/welcome/upgrade.html)【3:3†source】【3:5†source】.\\n\\n**3. Additional Cleanups:**\\n- Some outdated interfaces (like the ISO geometry interfaces) have been removed.\\n- Internationalized error message support was dropped.\\n- Cleanup of envelope-related classes to resolve long-standing confusion.\\n\\n**4. Breaking Change:**\\n- This refactor is a breaking API change. All downstream and third-party libraries using GeoTools will need to adjust their codebases.\\n  \\n**5.
